# Add an RGBA raster array to a pain drawing based on spray coordinates

Renders the strokes and spray points from one or more pain drawings into
RGBA numeric arrays. By default, the array is attached as a `$.rgba`
element to each drawing and the augmented drawing(s) are returned. Set
`rgba_only = TRUE` to return only the array(s), each wrapped in a named
list with a single `$.rgba` element. This is useful when assigning into
a separate tibble list-column.

## Usage

``` r
pdr_add_rgba(
  pdr_list,
  method = "memory",
  clean_up = TRUE,
  polygon = "none",
  rgba_only = FALSE
)
```

## Arguments

- pdr_list:

  A pain drawing in one of two forms:

  - A single drawing: a named list containing a `.strokes` element, as
    produced by
    [`pdr_import_json()`](https://steenharsted.github.io/paindrawr/reference/pdr_import_json.md)
    or extracted from a list-column (e.g. `pd$pdr_data[[1]]`).

  - A list of drawings of any length, including 1 (e.g.
    `pdr_example_data` or a list-column such as `pd$pdr_data`).

- method:

  Character. Controls the rasterization back-end. Either:

  - `"memory"` (default) — renders in-memory via
    [`ragg::agg_capture()`](https://ragg.r-lib.org/reference/agg_capture.html),
    no temp file is written to disk.

  - `"file"` — saves to a temporary PNG via
    [`ggplot2::ggsave()`](https://ggplot2.tidyverse.org/reference/ggsave.html)
    and reads it back with
    [`png::readPNG()`](https://rdrr.io/pkg/png/man/readPNG.html). Use
    this if the `"memory"` path is slow.

- clean_up:

  Logical. If `TRUE` (default), the temporary PNG file is deleted on
  exit. Only relevant when `method = "file"`. Set to `FALSE` to retain
  the file for debugging.

- polygon:

  Character. Controls whether strokes are rendered as filled polygons
  instead of their original appearance. One of:

  - `"none"` (default) — pen strokes as paths, spray strokes as points.

  - `"pen"` — pen strokes as filled polygons.

  - `"spray"` — spray strokes as filled polygons.

  - `"both"` — both pen and spray strokes as filled polygons.

- rgba_only:

  Logical. If `FALSE` (default), returns the input drawing(s) with a
  `$.rgba` element appended to each. If `TRUE`, returns a named list
  with a single `$.rgba` element (single drawing) or a list of such
  named lists (list of drawings). Use `TRUE` when assigning into a
  separate tibble list-column:
  `mutate(rgba = pdr_add_rgba(pdr_data, rgba_only = TRUE))`.

## Value

- `rgba_only = FALSE` (default): the input drawing (named list) with
  `$.rgba` appended, or a list of such augmented drawings when a list of
  drawings is supplied.

- `rgba_only = TRUE`: a named list with a single element `$.rgba`, or a
  list of such named lists when a list of drawings is supplied.

In both cases `$.rgba` is a numeric array of dimensions
`height × width × 4` (RGBA channels, values in \[0, 1\]). Transparent
pixels are set to black (`RGB = 0`).

## Details

The output always has the same shape as the input: a single drawing
returns a single result, and a list of drawings (of any length,
including 1) returns a list of results of the same length.

## Examples

``` r
# Single drawing: returns the drawing with $.rgba appended
drawing_with_rgba <- pdr_example_data$pdr_data[[1]] |> pdr_add_rgba()
grid::grid.newpage()
grid::grid.raster(drawing_with_rgba$.rgba)


# Single drawing: returns list(.rgba = <array>)
rgba <- pdr_example_data$pdr_data[[1]] |> pdr_add_rgba(rgba_only = TRUE)
dim(rgba$.rgba)
#> [1] 500 450   4

# Render spray strokes as filled polygons
pdr_example_data$pdr_data[[1]] |> pdr_add_rgba(polygon = "spray")
#> $.id
#> [1] "6d63804892233bbb18bc21bcf0d81d0d"

#> 
#> $.height

# \donttest{
# Multiple drawings at once (a pdr_list)
drawings_with_rgba <- pdr_example_data$pdr_data[1:3] |> pdr_add_rgba()

# Tibble workflow
pd <- pdr_example_data[1:3, ]

## add .rgba to the drawings in the pdr_data list-column
pd |> dplyr::mutate(pdr_data = pdr_add_rgba(pdr_data))
#> # A tibble: 3 × 6
#>   id                               pdr_data          sex      age duration  pain
#>   <chr>                            <list>            <fct>  <dbl> <fct>    <dbl>
#> 1 6d63804892233bbb18bc21bcf0d81d0d <named list [11]> Female  46   Chronic      6
#> 2 c8b12cd45d08e94241d4de9990d60f71 <named list [11]> Female  35.4 Acute        6
#> 3 25062ac7dbeb08d8b532ac474a7849ca <named list [11]> Female  38.4 Acute        5

## add rgba arrays in a separate list-column
pd_rgba <- pd |>
  dplyr::mutate(
    rgba = pdr_add_rgba(pdr_data, rgba_only = TRUE)
  )

## access the array for the first row
dim(pd_rgba$rgba[[1]]$.rgba)
#> [1] 500 450   4
# }
```
